# Signal Spec

This file is the only source of signal formulas (AD-19). The engine (`engine/`) and the Python reference (`reference/`) each implement it on their own; neither defines a formula of its own, and neither is ported from the other. A shape or stage not in this file does not exist.

Story 0.2 writes the core and sine sections. Story 1.2 writes the internal block size and the stub chain. Story 1.4 writes 24-bit rounding. Story 1.5 adds the theta reference oscillator to the stub chain. Story 2.1 writes the theta reference (envelope shapes, depth law). Story 2.2 writes the gamma PAC layer, uncoupled (AM law, constant depth). Story 2.3 writes coupling (coupling phase, smooth swell). Story 2.5 writes the binaural layer and the rate curve (beat rate cycle, closed-form phase integral). Later stories add bursts, ramps, rate curves on the theta and gamma rates, loop seams, the limiter, the resampler, the loudness window, and the spectral component model.

**Conventions:**

- **binary64:** IEEE 754 double precision, round to nearest, ties to even, no fused multiply-add (`-ffp-contract=off`, AD-9). Every binary64 operation written here is one correctly rounded operation, evaluated in the order written.
- **Hex floats:** binary64 constants are written as C99 hex float literals, so both sides start from the same bits.
- **Exact:** "exact" means integer or rational arithmetic with no rounding.
- **Worked vectors:** every vector table below is generated by `tools/signal_spec_vectors.py` from exact arithmetic (`fractions`) and 60-digit decimal arithmetic (`decimal`), never from the engine, and lives once in `tests/data/signal_spec_vectors.json`. A test checks that each table here equals that file. The engine test (`tests/cpp/`) and the Python ideal test (`tests/python/test_signal_spec_core.py`) each pass every vector on their own. To change a vector, change the generator, run it, and paste its `--tables` output here.

## Core

### Phase accumulator

Every oscillator keeps its phase in an unsigned 64-bit integer. One cycle is 2^64 phase units, so phase `p` stands for the fraction `p / 2^64` of a cycle.

```
phase[0]   = start phase (0 unless a later section says otherwise)
phase[n+1] = (phase[n] + inc) mod 2^64
```

The addition wraps modulo 2^64 (unsigned overflow). Phase is never stored in floating point. After `n` samples the phase is exactly `(phase[0] + n * inc) mod 2^64`, so phase error never accumulates beyond the error in `inc` itself.

### Phase increment rule

For a frequency `f_hz` (a binary64 value, in Hz) at sample rate `fs` (a positive integer, 44100 or 48000, held exactly in binary64):

```
q   = (f_hz * 0x1p64) / fs      binary64: the multiply is exact, the division rounds once
inc = q rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even, as an unsigned 64-bit integer
```

Domain: `0 <= f_hz < fs / 2`. Any other `f_hz`, NaN and both infinities included, gives `inc = 0`. The rule never fails or throws. Negative zero is in the domain and gives `inc = 0`.

An exact tie (`q` exactly k + 1/2) can happen whenever `q` is below 2^52, that is for `f_hz` below about `fs / 4096` (about 10.8 Hz at 44.1 kHz and 11.7 Hz at 48 kHz), which includes the theta rate band; at or above 2^52 every binary64 `q` is already an integer. The tie vectors below exercise it, both at tiny frequencies and in the 1 to 6 Hz band. `inc` is at most 2^63, so it always fits.

**Frequency error.** The frequency the accumulator plays is `f_q = inc * fs / 2^64`. Two errors make up `|f_q - f_hz|`: the division rounding (at most half a unit in the last place of `q`) and the integer rounding (at most 1/2). For `f_hz` up to 1030 Hz, `q` is below 2^59, so `|inc - f_hz * 2^64 / fs|` is at most 32.5 and `|f_q - f_hz|` is at most `32.5 * fs / 2^64`, about 8.5e-14 Hz at 48 kHz.

**Drift.** Over a session of `T` seconds the timing error of a signal at `f_hz > 0` is `|f_q - f_hz| * T / f_hz` seconds. At 0 Hz, `inc = 0` and there is no drift.

### Seconds-to-samples rule

Every time value in a recipe or in this spec is in seconds and converts to a sample count by one rule (AD-10):

```
x = t_s * fs                    binary64, one rounding
n = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even, as an unsigned 64-bit integer
```

Domain: `t_s >= 0` and finite, with `x < 2^64`. Any other `t_s` (negative, NaN, infinite, or one whose product reaches 2^64) gives `n = 0`.

## Sine

### Ideal sine

The ideal sine of a phase is

```
y = sin(2 * pi * phase / 2^64)
```

evaluated exactly (in real numbers). Sample `n` of an unmodulated sine oscillator is the sine of `phase[n]`, read before the increment is added. The Python reference renders the ideal sine, never the engine approximation (design log 2026-10-28 (o)).

### Engine sine

The engine owns its sine (AD-9: no libm). For a phase `p`:

1. **Fold, exact integer steps.** Read `p` as a signed 64-bit two's-complement integer `s`, in `[-2^63, 2^63)`, so `s / 2^64` is the signed fraction of a cycle. Fold into `[-2^62, 2^62]` with `sin(pi - x) = sin(x)`:
   - if `s > 2^62`: `s = 2^63 - s`
   - if `s < -2^62`: `s = -2^63 - s`
   - otherwise `s` is unchanged.

   Each step is exact in 64-bit integer arithmetic (compute it in unsigned arithmetic modulo 2^64 and read the result as signed).
2. **Convert once.** `x = double(s) * 0x1.921fb54442d18p-62`. `double(s)` is the one int64-to-binary64 conversion (round to nearest, ties to even). The constant is the binary64 nearest to pi, scaled by 2^-63 (exact; equivalently pi/2 times 2^-62), so it is the binary64 nearest to `2 * pi / 2^64`; the multiply is one rounding. `x` lies in `[-pi/2, pi/2]`.
3. **Odd Taylor polynomial to x^11, fixed Horner order.** With the coefficients below:

   ```
   z = x * x
   r = C11
   r = r * z + C9
   r = r * z + C7
   r = r * z + C5
   r = r * z + C3
   r = r * z + C1
   y = r * x
   ```

   Each `*` and `+` is one binary64 operation (no fused multiply-add). `C1` is exactly 1.

The engine sine returns `y` as binary64. Converting it to float32 for output is a separate step, round to nearest, ties to even.

**Coefficients.** `Ck` is the binary64 nearest to `±1/k!` (sign alternating, `+` for k = 1). They are exact-derivable, so no fitted constant needs a source.

| Power | Exact | Binary64 (hex float) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | +1/1! | `0x1.0000000000000p+0` |
| 3 | -1/3! | `-0x1.5555555555555p-3` |
| 5 | +1/5! | `0x1.1111111111111p-7` |
| 7 | -1/7! | `-0x1.a01a01a01a01ap-13` |
| 9 | +1/9! | `0x1.71de3a556c734p-19` |
| 11 | -1/11! | `-0x1.ae64567f544e4p-26` |

### Error bound

```
20 * log10( max over the test phases of |engine sine(p) - ideal sine(p)| ) <= -120 dBFS
```

Full scale is 1.0. Source: design log 2026-10-28 (o) (engineering default; margin under the -100 dBFS reference match). Truncation error of the polynomial on `[-pi/2, pi/2]` is at most `(pi/2)^13 / 13!`, about 5.7e-8 (-145 dBFS), so the bound holds with about 25 dB to spare before rounding.

**Test resolution.** The test phases are the 2^24 evenly spaced phases `k * 2^40` for `k = 0 .. 2^24 - 1` (one full cycle), plus each quadrant edge `0, 2^62, 2^63, 3 * 2^62` and its neighbours `edge - 1` and `edge + 1`, modulo 2^64.

### Vectors: exact phases

At these phases the ideal sine is exact. The engine sine must be within the bound of these values.

| Phase | Ideal sine |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | 0 |
| 2^61 | 0.7071067811865475244008444 |
| 2^62 | 1 |
| 2^63 | 0 |
| 3*2^62 | -1 |

## Internal block size

The engine renders in internal blocks of 64 frames. A frame is one sample per channel (left, then right). The block size is a fixed engine constant, not a recipe parameter: it never shapes the signal. Source: engineering default, design log 2026-11-04 (gg).

```
block_frames = 64
block(k)     = k div 64        frame k lies in this block
offset(k)    = k mod 64        at this offset inside it
```

Every sample is a pure function of the plan and its sample index `n` (AD-10). A host request of any length is served from the internal blocks, so neither the block size nor the host's request size changes a sample. Story 1.6 proves block-size invariance.

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets every oscillator phase to exactly `(phase[0] + k * inc) mod 2^64`, the same value sample-by-sample stepping reaches, and the next rendered sample is sample `k`. Seek reads no earlier output, so it is pure. Seeking to any `k`, including one past the session end, is allowed.

### Vectors: internal blocks

| Frame | Block | Offset in block |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 37 | 0 | 37 |

## Stub chain

The fixed stage list of Story 1.2, before any real layer exists. Story 1.5 routes the sine through the theta reference stub as an unmodulated carrier; Epic 2 replaces the theta reference (Story 2.1, Theta reference), gamma PAC (Story 2.2, Gamma PAC layer), and binaural (Story 2.5, Binaural layer) stubs with the real layers, and Epic 4 replaces the stored gain and the limiter stub.

**Stage order.** Theta reference, gamma PAC layer, stereo bus, binaural join, master chain (per-channel gain, then a stereo-linked limiter). The order is fixed and is not a recipe field. Every stage writes channel `c` only from channel `c` of its inputs. The limiter alone reads both channels, and only to derive one linked gain that it applies to each channel separately. No stage reads one channel to write the other.

**Session length.** `N = seconds_to_samples(length_s, fs)` frames (Core, seconds-to-samples rule). For `n >= N` every tap and the output are exactly `+0.0` in both channels.

**Theta reference oscillator (Story 1.5).** Story 2.1 replaces this stub with the real layer (Theta reference). At `am_depth_percent` 0 the real layer is this stub bit for bit, so the stub chain vectors below hold unchanged; the fixture `stub-chain-1s-beat-6.json` sets `rate_hz` 6, `am_depth_percent` 0, and `shape` `sine`. The Story 1.5 stub, with a `theta_reference` field, is one unmodulated sine at the theta carrier, the same value in both channels:

```
f_T   = theta_reference.carrier_hz
inc_T = phase increment rule (f_T, fs)
phase_T[0] = 0,  phase_T[n] = n * inc_T mod 2^64
a_T   = 1.0                                 unit amplitude
```

A recipe without a `theta_reference` field has `a_T = 0.0` and `inc_T = 0`. The stub has no amplitude modulation, envelope, or level of its own: the theta-rate modulation and the envelopes arrive in Epic 2, and the stored gain in Epic 4. For the fixture `stub-chain-1s-beat-6.json` it is a 300 Hz unmodulated sine in each channel; `stub-chain-1s-beat-0.json` has no `theta_reference` field.

**Binaural oscillators.** Story 2.5 replaces this stub with the real layer (Binaural layer, and Rate curve for the beat rate cycle). Without a `beat_rate_cycle` field the real layer is this stub bit for bit (its center carrier `f_C` is `f_R` here), so the stub chain vectors below hold unchanged. The stub is one unmodulated sine per ear:

```
f_L   = carrier_left_hz
f_R   = carrier_left_hz + beat_rate_hz      binary64, one rounding (range row binaural-carrier-right)
inc_c = phase increment rule (f_c, fs)      c = L or R
phase_c[0] = 0,  phase_c[n] = n * inc_c mod 2^64
a_c   = 1.0                                 unit amplitude
```

For the fixture `stub-chain-1s-beat-6.json` that is a 400 Hz unmodulated sine in the left ear and a 406 Hz unmodulated sine in the right ear, a 6 Hz binaural beat; for `stub-chain-1s-beat-0.json` it is a 400 Hz unmodulated sine in each ear. A recipe without a `binaural` field has `a_L = a_R = 0.0` and `inc_L = inc_R = 0`.

**Muted ear.** The compile option `mute_binaural_ear` (none, left, or right) is an offline measurement control for the isolation test (FR-4), stored in the plan; it is not a recipe field. For the muted ear `c`, the plan holds `inc_c = 0` and `a_c = 0.0`, so the render path has no test branch and that ear's binaural oscillator outputs `0.0 * sine(0) = +0.0`. Muting affects only the binaural oscillator: the theta reference and the gamma PAC layer are unchanged, so the muted channel's `join`, `master`, and `out` equal its stereo bus (`theta + gamma`) bit for bit, which is the theta tap when there is no `gamma_pac` field. The engine bus is never `-0.0` (the engine gamma tap never is, and `-0.0 + +0.0` is `+0.0`), so adding the muted ear's `+0.0` changes no bit.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < N` and each channel `c`, in this order, each line one binary64 operation (Conventions):

```
theta[c][n]  = a[n] * sine(phase_T[n])      theta reference (Theta reference, Per sample): the same value in both channels
gamma[c][n]  = 0.0                          gamma PAC stub (Gamma PAC layer, Per sample: a[n] * sine(phase_G[n]) with a gamma_pac field)
bus[c][n]    = theta[c][n] + gamma[c][n]    stereo bus: adds the layers within channel c
bin[c][n]    = a_c * sine(phase_c[n])       binaural oscillator of ear c (Sine, engine sine)
join[c][n]   = bus[c][n] + bin[c][n]        binaural join: ear c is added to channel c only
master[c][n] = join[c][n] * g_c             per-channel gain, g_L = g_R = 1.0 (stored gain is Epic 4)
out[c][n]    = master[c][n]                 stereo-linked limiter stub: identity, zero gain reduction
```

`y * 1.0 = y` exactly for every binary64 `y`, and `y + +0.0 = y` and `+0.0 + y = y` exactly for every binary64 `y` other than `-0.0` (where the sum is `+0.0`). No stub chain fixture produces `-0.0` (the theta reference can only at 100 percent depth; Theta reference, Per sample): the engine sine (Sine, Engine sine) returns zero only for a folded `s = 0`, where `x = +0.0`, `r = C1 = 1` and `y = 1 * +0.0 = +0.0`, and is otherwise nonzero with the sign of `s`, since `r` stays positive on `[-pi/2, pi/2]`; an absent theta reference, a muted ear, or an absent ear is `0.0 * sine(0) = +0.0`. No stub chain fixture has a `gamma_pac` field, so `gamma` is `+0.0` and `bus = theta` bit for bit; with a gamma layer the bus is the one-rounding sum (Gamma PAC layer, Stereo bus). `join = bus + bin` is one rounding; it is exact, and equals the other operand, when `theta` or `bin` is `+0.0` (no `theta_reference` field, or a muted or absent ear). `master` and `out` equal `join` bit for bit.

**Level.** Both stub oscillators run at unit amplitude, so a recipe with a theta reference and a binaural layer peaks near 2.0. `stub-chain-1s-beat-6.json` (300 Hz theta, 400 and 406 Hz binaural) does, and its 24-bit WAV clips by the 24-bit rounding rule (24-bit rounding). It is a test fixture, not for listening; real levels arrive in Epic 2 and Epic 4.

**Output and taps.** The render output is `out`, interleaved stereo binary64 (left, then right, per frame). Five optional taps, each interleaved stereo binary64 over the same frames: theta reference (`theta`), gamma PAC (`gamma`), stereo bus (`bus`), binaural join (`join`, the engine output point of the isolation row `binaural-isolation-engine`), and master (`out`, the point of row `binaural-isolation-master`).

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets `phase_T` to `k * inc_T mod 2^64`, the same rule as the binaural phases (Internal block size, Seek).

### Vectors: stub chain samples

Per channel at sample `n`: the theta tap (the same in both channels, and equal to the stereo bus tap) and `out` (and so `join` and `master`), as the 64-bit pattern of the binary64 value (big-endian hex), from the engine sine bits. For the Python reference (Story 1.5), the ideal values of the same phases at 25 significant digits: the theta ideal is the ideal sine of the theta phase (`0` without a theta reference), and the left and right ideal is the exact sum of the theta ideal and the ideal sine of that ear's binaural phase, rounded once. Indices cover the start, inside the first block (37), both sides of block boundaries (63, 64, 65, 127, 128), the last sample, and two samples past the end (case `past_end`, output and taps `+0.0`; the phase columns there show `n * inc mod 2^64` only for reference).

## Theta reference

Story 2.1 replaces the theta reference stub (Stub chain) with the real layer: a carrier amplitude-modulated at the theta rate by one of four envelope shapes, the same value in both channels. It is the only signal the gamma PAC layer may couple to, so its phase exists in the file and is measurable.

**Recipe fields.** All four are required in `theta_reference`:

- `carrier_hz`, the carrier in Hz (range row `theta-carrier`)
- `rate_hz`, the theta rate in Hz (range row `theta-rate`)
- `am_depth_percent`, the AM depth in percent (range row `theta-depth`)
- `shape`, one of `sine`, `smoothed_on_off`, `sawtooth`, `reverse_saw`

The schema also accepts `shape: "custom"` so that compile can reject it by name: parameter `/theta_reference/shape`, value `custom`, rule `shape not modeled in the signal spec`, limit `sine, smoothed_on_off, sawtooth, reverse_saw`. A custom envelope curve does not exist until this section models it. Any other shape text is a schema fault (rule `enum`). The carrier rule (row `carrier-rule`) checks `carrier_hz` against `rate_hz`; every pair inside the locked ranges passes it, since 100 Hz is at least 4 times 6 Hz.

**Oscillators.** Two phase accumulators (Core), each at phase 0 at n = 0:

```
inc_T = phase increment rule (carrier_hz, fs)    carrier phase:  phase_T[n] = n * inc_T mod 2^64
inc_E = phase increment rule (rate_hz, fs)       envelope phase: phase_E[n] = n * inc_E mod 2^64
```

`phi = phase_E / 2^64` is the fraction of the envelope cycle. `phi = 0` is the shape's 0 degree point, its peak, so the 0 degree point of the first cycle is sample 0. Coupling phases (Coupling) are measured from this point.

**Fixed constants.** Two engineering defaults, decided by Audio on 2026-11-06. No study sets them. Each is a fixed range row (min equal to max), like `burst-width` and `burst-edge`, and neither is a recipe field:

- on share `D`, row `theta-on-share`, 50 percent: `D = 50.0 / 100.0` (binary64, exact)
- edge length `E`, row `theta-edge`, 10 ms: `E = 10.0 / 1000.0` seconds (binary64, one rounding, `0x1.47ae147ae147bp-7`). 10 ms matches the gamma burst edges, the top of the 5 to 10 ms rise-time convention (design log 2026-10-28 (o)).

They become integer phase lengths once per plan:

```
H = D * 0x1p63       binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale); then the nearest integer, ties to even
w = E * rate_hz      binary64, one rounding
W = w * 0x1p64       binary64, exact; then the nearest integer, ties to even
```

`H` is half the on share in phase units (2^62). `W` is `E` seconds of envelope phase at `rate_hz`, in phase units. Over the locked rate range `W` is at most about `0.06 * 2^64`, so `W < H - W` and every edge fits inside its segment: at 6 Hz, the top theta rate, the smoothed on-off plateau is still 0.19 of a cycle on each side of the 0 degree point (0.38 in all) and the sawtooth's linear segment 0.94.

**Ratio-to-phase rule.** The edges convert a ratio `r` to a half-cycle phase by one clamped conversion:

```
if not (r > 0):  P = 0          NaN, both zeros, negatives
else if r >= 1:  P = 2^63       1, everything above, +inf
else:            x = r * 0x1p63          binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale)
                 P = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even, as an unsigned 64-bit integer
```

**Half raised cosine.** For an integer offset `u` with `0 <= u <= W`, the fall from 1 to 0 across one edge:

```
ideal:   rc(u) = (1 + cos(pi * u / W)) / 2
engine:  r  = double(u) / double(W)          two int-to-binary64 conversions, one division
         P  = ratio-to-phase rule (r)
         c  = sine(P + 2^62 mod 2^64)        engine sine (Sine): sine(p + 2^62) is the cosine of p
         rc = (1.0 + c) * 0.5                one rounding, then an exact halving
```

**Envelope shapes.** The envelope `e` of the envelope phase `p = phase_E[n]` lies in [0, 1]. Each shape is a set of segments chosen by exact integer comparisons on `p`:

- **sine:** `e = (1 + cos(2 * pi * p / 2^64)) / 2`. Engine: `c = sine(p + 2^62 mod 2^64)`, then `e = (1.0 + c) * 0.5`. Peak at `p = 0`, trough at `p = 2^63`.
- **smoothed_on_off:** on for the share `D` of the cycle centered on `p = 0`, off for the rest, with raised-cosine edges of length `E` inside the on share. With `m = p` when `p < 2^63` and `m = 2^64 - p` otherwise (exact unsigned arithmetic; `m` is the distance from the 0 degree point, 2^63 at the half cycle):
  - `m <= H - W`: `e = 1.0` (plateau)
  - `H - W < m < H`: `e = rc(m - (H - W))` (the falling edge after the plateau, and its mirror, the rising edge before it)
  - `m >= H`: `e = 0.0` (off)
- **sawtooth:** a raised-cosine drop of length `E` starting at `p = 0`, then a linear rise to 1 at `p = 2^64`:
  - `p < W`: `e = rc(p)` (drop edge)
  - `p >= W`: `e = double(p - W) / double(2^64 - W)` (linear rise; both differences exact in unsigned 64-bit arithmetic, then two conversions and one division). The ideal is the exact ratio.
- **reverse_saw:** the mirror image of the sawtooth about `p = 0`: a linear fall from 1 at `p = 0`, then a raised-cosine rise of length `E` ending at `p = 2^64`. `e_reverse_saw(p) = e_sawtooth(2^64 - p mod 2^64)`, the argument exact in unsigned arithmetic.

Every engine envelope value lies in [0, 1]: the engine sine stays strictly inside (-1, 1) (its largest magnitude is `0x1.fffffe1cbd435p-1`, at 2^62 and 3 * 2^62), and each linear ratio's numerator is at most its denominator, both converted by the same monotone rounding. The engine envelope is exactly 1.0 only on the smoothed on-off plateau and where a linear ratio rounds to 1. At the 0 degree point of the sine, sawtooth, and reverse saw it is `(1.0 + sine(2^62)) * 0.5`, about 1.4e-8 below 1, inside the error bound below.

**Depth law.** `d = am_depth_percent / 100.0` (binary64, one rounding), once per plan. Per sample:

```
a[n] = 1.0 - d * (1.0 - e[n])      three binary64 operations, in this order
```

So `a = 1` at the envelope peak and `a = 1 - d` where `e = 0`: 75 percent dips the carrier to a quarter of its peak (design log 2026-10-29 (s)). At `d = 0`, `d * (1.0 - e)` is `+0.0` for every `e` in [0, 1], so `a = 1.0` exactly and a depth 0 theta reference is the Story 1.5 unmodulated carrier, bit for bit.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < N` and each channel `c`, this line replaces the theta reference stub line of Stub chain, Per sample; every other line there is unchanged:

```
theta[c][n] = a[n] * sine(phase_T[n])      the same value in both channels (Sine, engine sine)
```

Without a `theta_reference` field, `theta[c][n] = +0.0`. The theta tap is `-0.0` only where `a[n] = 0.0` (depth 100 percent where `e = 0`) and the carrier sine is negative; there the stereo bus, `theta + gamma`, is `+0.0` without a gamma layer, equal in value. Everywhere else, without a gamma layer, the bus equals the theta tap bit for bit (Gamma PAC layer, Stereo bus, covers the sum).

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets `phase_T` to `k * inc_T mod 2^64` and `phase_E` to `k * inc_E mod 2^64`.

**Reference.** The Python reference renders the same segments, chosen by the same integer comparisons on the same integer phases, with `H`, `W`, and both increments from the rules above. It evaluates the ideal functions in binary64: `cos(pi * u / W)` and `cos(2 * pi * p / 2^64)` with NumPy, each linear ratio as one binary64 division, the depth law in the order above, and the ideal sine (Sine) for the carrier.

**Error bound.** With `b` the sine error bound (row `sine-error`), the theta tap uses two engine sines per sample: one for the envelope (sine shape and edges; none on a plateau, off, or linear segment) and one for the carrier. Halving the cosine halves its error, and the ratio conversions move the edge phase by a few units in the last place, so `|e - e_ideal| <= b / 2 + 2^-48`. Then `|a - a_ideal| <= d * (b / 2 + 2^-48)` and `|theta - theta_ideal| <= 1.5 * b + 2^-47`, about -116 dBFS, 16 dB under row `reference-match`. An output that also adds the binaural sines uses three sines per sample and stays within `2.5 * b + 2^-46`, about -112 dBFS.

### Vectors: theta ratio to phase

`r` and `x` are hex floats; `x` is `none` where the clamp returns first.

| Ratio case | r (hex) | x = r * 2^63 (hex) | P |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| zero | `0x0.0p+0` | `none` | 0 |
| negative zero | `-0x0.0p+0` | `none` | 0 |
| NaN | `nan` | `none` | 0 |
| negative | `-0x1.0000000000000p-1` | `none` | 0 |
| tie x = 1/2, down to even | `0x1.0000000000000p-64` | `0x1.0000000000000p-1` | 0 |
| tie x = 3/2, up to even | `0x1.8000000000000p-63` | `0x1.8000000000000p+0` | 2 |
| tie x = 5/2, down to even | `0x1.4000000000000p-62` | `0x1.4000000000000p+1` | 2 |
| quarter | `0x1.0000000000000p-2` | `0x1.0000000000000p+61` | 2305843009213693952 |
| half | `0x1.0000000000000p-1` | `0x1.0000000000000p+62` | 4611686018427387904 |
| one third (inexact in binary64) | `0x1.5555555555555p-2` | `0x1.5555555555555p+61` | 3074457345618258432 |
| largest binary64 below 1 | `0x1.fffffffffffffp-1` | `0x1.fffffffffffffp+62` | 9223372036854774784 |
| one | `0x1.0000000000000p+0` | `none` | 9223372036854775808 |
| above one | `0x1.8000000000000p+0` | `none` | 9223372036854775808 |
| infinity | `inf` | `none` | 9223372036854775808 |

## Gamma PAC layer

Story 2.2 replaces the gamma PAC stub (Stub chain) with the real layer, uncoupled: a separate sine carrier amplitude-modulated at the gamma rate with a constant AM depth, the same value in both channels. Stories 2.3 and 2.4 add the coupled settings, in which the depth follows the theta reference phase; the uncoupled layer is the control they are compared with. Uncoupled gamma reads nothing from the theta reference.

**Recipe fields.** These five are required in `gamma_pac`:

- `carrier_hz`, the gamma carrier in Hz (range row `gamma-carrier`)
- `rate_hz`, the gamma rate in Hz (range row `gamma-rate`)
- `am_depth_percent`, the AM depth in percent (range row `coupling-depth`)
- `carrier_wave`, one of `sine`, `triangle`, `softened_square`
- `coupling`, one of `uncoupled` or `smooth_swell` (Coupling; Story 2.4 adds `bursts`)

and one optional member, `coupling_phase_deg` (Coupling), after `coupling`.

The schema also accepts `triangle` and `softened_square` so that compile can reject them by name: parameter `/gamma_pac/carrier_wave`, value the text, rule `non-sine carrier not modeled`, limit `sine`. No non-sine carrier exists until this section models it. Any other carrier wave or coupling text is a schema fault (rule `enum`). The carrier rule (row `carrier-rule`) checks `carrier_hz` against `rate_hz`. Not every pair inside the locked ranges passes it: at a 60 Hz rate the carrier must be at least 240 Hz, above the 200 Hz floor of `gamma-carrier`.

**Compile order.** After every range check, compile runs these rules and the first failure wins: the theta reference carrier rule, the theta shape rule (Theta reference), the gamma carrier rule (parameter `/gamma_pac/carrier_hz`), then the carrier wave rule. All of them run before `final.required`. The coupling rules (Coupling, Compile order) follow the carrier wave rule.

**Oscillators.** Two phase accumulators (Core), each at phase 0 at n = 0:

```
inc_G = phase increment rule (carrier_hz, fs)    carrier phase:  phase_G[n] = n * inc_G mod 2^64
inc_M = phase increment rule (rate_hz, fs)       envelope phase: phase_M[n] = n * inc_M mod 2^64
```

`phi = phase_M / 2^64` is the fraction of the gamma envelope cycle; `phi = 0` is its peak, so the first gamma envelope peak is sample 0.

**AM law.** The envelope is the theta reference sine shape (Theta reference, Envelope shapes) at the gamma rate:

```
ideal:   e = (1 + cos(2 * pi * phase_M / 2^64)) / 2
engine:  c = sine(phase_M + 2^62 mod 2^64)      engine sine (Sine): sine(p + 2^62) is the cosine of p
         e = (1.0 + c) * 0.5                    one rounding, then an exact halving
```

`d = am_depth_percent / 100.0` (binary64, one rounding), once per plan. Per sample:

```
a[n] = 1.0 - d * (1.0 - e[n])      three binary64 operations, in this order
```

**Uncoupled.** `d` is the same for every sample of the render; nothing else sets it. So `a = 1` at each envelope peak and `a = 1 - d` at each trough: 100 percent dips the carrier to silence, 25 percent to three quarters of its peak. At `d = 0`, `a = 1.0` exactly and the layer is an unmodulated sine at the gamma carrier. With `smooth_swell` the depth follows the theta reference phase instead (Coupling, Depth law, coupled).

The engine envelope is never 0: the engine sine stays strictly inside (-1, 1) (Theta reference, Envelope shapes), so `1.0 + c > 0`, and at the trough `e` is about 2.8e-8 (`0x3e5e342bcb000000`). With `d` in [0, 1], `a >= e > 0` for every sample.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < N` and each channel `c`, this line replaces the gamma PAC stub line of Stub chain, Per sample; every other line there is unchanged:

```
gamma[c][n] = a[n] * sine(phase_G[n])      the same value in both channels (Sine, engine sine)
```

Without a `gamma_pac` field, `gamma[c][n] = +0.0`. Since `a > 0`, the engine gamma tap is `+0.0` only where the carrier sine is `+0.0` (a folded phase of 0) and is never `-0.0`.

**Stereo bus.** `bus[c][n] = theta[c][n] + gamma[c][n]` is one rounding. Without a theta reference, `theta` is `+0.0` and the bus equals the gamma tap bit for bit. With both layers it is the rounded sum. The Python reference's envelope can be exactly 0.0 at 100 percent depth (its binary64 cosine rounds to -1 near the trough), so its gamma tap can be `-0.0` where the carrier sine is negative. There its bus is `theta + -0.0`: the theta tap bit for bit when the theta tap is nonzero or `-0.0`, and `+0.0` when it is `+0.0`. In every case the bus equals the sum of the two taps in value.

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets `phase_G` to `k * inc_G mod 2^64` and `phase_M` to `k * inc_M mod 2^64`.

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/gamma.py`, implemented from this section) uses the same integer phases and the same increments. It evaluates the ideal envelope in binary64 with NumPy (the cosine of the signed envelope phase times `2 * pi / 2^64`), the depth law in the order above, and the ideal sine (Sine) for the carrier. With `smooth_swell` it also evaluates the swell (Coupling, Reference).

**Error bound.** With `b` the sine error bound (row `sine-error`), the margin of each tap is stated by its count of engine sine evaluations per sample:

- **Gamma tap: 2 sines** (the envelope cosine and the carrier; uncoupled, Coupling states the smooth swell bounds). Halving the cosine halves its error, so `|e - e_ideal| <= b / 2 + 2^-52`, `|a - a_ideal| <= d * (b / 2 + 2^-52) + 2^-52`, and `|gamma - gamma_ideal| <= 1.5 * b + 2^-47`, about -116 dBFS, the theta tap's bound.
- **Stereo bus.** Without a theta reference it is the gamma tap (2 sines). With one it adds the theta tap's 2 sines, 4 in all: within `3 * b + 2^-45`, about -110 dBFS.
- **Output.** The binaural join adds that ear's binaural sine, 5 in all with both layers: within `4 * b + 2^-44`, about -108 dBFS.

Every bound is under row `reference-match` (-100 dBFS).

**Level.** The gamma layer runs at unit amplitude, like the theta reference; level fields arrive in Epic 4. `theta-gamma-uncoupled.json` (both layers) peaks near 2.0, and its 24-bit WAV clips by the 24-bit rounding rule. It is a test fixture, not for listening.

### Vectors: gamma envelope

The engine envelope `e` and amplitude `a` (binary64 bits, from the engine sine bits) and their ideal values (25 significant digits: ideal cosine in decimal arithmetic, exact depth law on the binary64 `d`) at the envelope phases 0 (the peak), 1, 2^61 (an eighth of a cycle), 2^62, 2^63, and 3 * 2^62 (the quarter, half, and three-quarter points), and 2^64 - 1 (the cycle's last phase), at 0, 25, and 100 percent depth. The envelope does not depend on the rate.

### Vectors: gamma samples

Per fixture and sample rate at sample `n`: both gamma phases, and the engine envelope `e`, amplitude `a`, and gamma tap (the same in both channels) as binary64 bits, with the ideal envelope and ideal gamma tap at 25 digits (the exact depth law on the binary64 `d`, ideal cosine, ideal sine). For the gamma-only fixtures the gamma tap is also the stereo bus and the output. Indices cover the start (0 and 1), both sides of the first two block boundaries (63, 64, 65, 127, 128), sample 1000, both sides of the quarter, half, and three-quarter points of the first gamma envelope cycle, the last sample, and two samples past the end (case `past_end`: taps and output `+0.0`; the phase columns show `n * inc mod 2^64` only for reference).

## Coupling

Story 2.3 adds the first coupled setting of the gamma PAC layer (Gamma PAC layer), the smooth swell: the gamma AM depth rises and falls once per theta cycle, peaks at the recipe's coupling phase, and falls to 0 half a theta cycle away. Only the AM depth varies; the gamma carrier always plays. Story 2.4 adds the 50 ms bursts.

**Recipe fields.** Two members of `gamma_pac`:

- `coupling`, one of `uncoupled` or `smooth_swell` (Story 2.4 adds `bursts`)
- `coupling_phase_deg`, optional, placed after `coupling`: the coupling phase in degrees (range row `coupling-phase`, 0 to 360 inclusive). 360 is the same point as 0.

The coupling phase is measured on the theta reference envelope from its 0 degree point (Theta reference, Oscillators: `phase_E = 0`, the envelope peak of every shape), in the direction of increasing theta envelope phase (design log 2026-10-27 (b)). There is no coupling-target field: the theta reference is the only signal gamma may couple to (AD-5).

**Compile order.** After every range check, compile runs these rules and the first failure wins: the theta reference carrier rule and theta shape rule (Theta reference), the gamma carrier rule and the carrier wave rule (Gamma PAC layer), then:

1. **Coupled gamma needs the theta reference (AD-5).** With `coupling` `smooth_swell` and gamma `am_depth_percent` above 0, a recipe without a `theta_reference` field, or with theta `am_depth_percent` 0, is rejected: parameter `/gamma_pac/coupling`, value `smooth_swell`, rule `coupled gamma needs a theta reference with AM depth`, limit `theta_reference present with am_depth_percent > 0`. A gamma depth of 0 passes, theta reference or not: the swell then scales nothing.
2. **Coupled gamma needs a coupling phase.** `smooth_swell` without `coupling_phase_deg` is rejected: parameter `/gamma_pac/coupling_phase_deg`, value `absent`, rule `coupled gamma needs a coupling phase`, limit `present with coupling smooth_swell`.
3. **A coupling phase needs coupled gamma.** `uncoupled` with `coupling_phase_deg` is rejected: parameter `/gamma_pac/coupling_phase_deg`, value the phase (shortest round-trip text), rule `coupling phase needs coupled gamma`, limit `absent with coupling uncoupled`.

All of them run before `final.required`. A coupling phase outside the row is a range fault (rule `range.min` or `range.max`, limit for example `>= 0 degrees (row coupling-phase)`), found before these rules. Coupling phase text is a schema fault (rule `type`).

Story 4.4 adds the zero-level rejections, when level and fade fields arrive. Until then every layer runs at unit level, so no recipe can put the theta reference at zero level.

**Coupling phase rule.** The coupling phase becomes an integer phase once per plan:

```
x = coupling_phase_deg / 360.0      binary64, one rounding
C = x * 0x1p64                      binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale); then the nearest
                                    integer, ties to even, modulo 2^64
```

After the range check `x` lies in [0, 1]. Only `x = 1` (360 degrees) gives `x * 2^64 = 2^64`, and `C = 0` there, the same point as 0 degrees. Below 2^52 the product can have a fraction, so the rounding can tie (Vectors: coupling phase).

**Swell law.** One raised cosine per theta cycle, centered on `C`, the same for every theta shape: each shape's 0 degree point is at theta envelope phase 0 (Theta reference, Oscillators), so the coupling phase means the same point of the cycle for all four. With `p_E = phase_E[n]`, the theta envelope phase of the theta reference (Theta reference, Oscillators):

```
ideal:   s = (1 + cos(2 * pi * (p_E - C) / 2^64)) / 2
engine:  u = p_E - C mod 2^64                  exact in unsigned arithmetic
         s = sine_envelope(u)                  the gamma envelope function (Gamma PAC layer, AM law):
                                               c = sine(u + 2^62 mod 2^64), s = (1.0 + c) * 0.5
```

`s` is 1 where the theta envelope phase equals `C` and 0 half a theta cycle away. It reads `p_E` and the plan constant `C` only: never the theta carrier phase, theta tap samples, theta depth, or theta shape, never a binaural value, and nothing else. It has one peak per theta cycle and no edge, so no edge of a hard-edged theta shape (the sawtooth drop, the smoothed on-off edges) reaches the gamma depth. No study sets the swell shape; it is an engineering default (design log 2026-11-10 (nn)).

**Depth law, coupled.** `d = am_depth_percent / 100.0` (binary64, one rounding), once per plan, and `e` the gamma envelope (Gamma PAC layer, AM law). Per sample:

```
a[n] = 1.0 - (d * s[n]) * (1.0 - e[n])      four binary64 operations: d * s, then 1.0 - e,
                                            then their product, then 1.0 minus it
```

So the AM depth is `d * s`: `d` at the coupling phase and 0 half a theta cycle away. At the coupling phase `a` dips to `1 - d` at each gamma envelope trough; half a theta cycle away the carrier is unmodulated. At `d = 0`, `d * s` is `+0.0` for every `s` in [0, 1], so `a = 1.0` exactly and the layer is an unmodulated sine at the gamma carrier, theta reference or not. Without a `theta_reference` field `inc_E = 0`, so `p_E = 0` for every sample.

The engine swell is never 0 or 1: the engine sine stays strictly inside (-1, 1) (Theta reference, Envelope shapes), so `s` lies in about [2.8e-8, 1 - 1.4e-8]. With `d` in [0, 1] and `e > 0` (Gamma PAC layer), `(d * s) * (1.0 - e) < 1`, so `a > 0` for every sample and the engine gamma tap is never `-0.0`.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < N` and each channel `c`, with `smooth_swell` the gamma line of Gamma PAC layer, Per sample, takes the coupled `a[n]`:

```
gamma[c][n] = a[n] * sine(phase_G[n])      the same value in both channels (Sine, engine sine)
```

With `uncoupled` the gamma line is unchanged, so every uncoupled fixture keeps its bytes.

**Seek.** No new accumulator: the swell reads the theta envelope phase, which seek already sets to `k * inc_E mod 2^64` (Theta reference, Seek).

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/gamma.py`) takes the theta reference fields to derive `p_E = n * inc_E mod 2^64` (`inc_E` from the increment rule on `theta_reference.rate_hz`; 0 without a theta reference), computes `C` by the coupling phase rule, and evaluates `s` as the cosine of the signed phase `p_E - C mod 2^64`, in [-2^63, 2^63), times `2 * pi / 2^64` with NumPy, then the depth law in the order above, in binary64.

**Error bound.** With `b` the sine error bound (row `sine-error`), the margin of each tap is stated by its count of engine sine evaluations per sample:

- **Gamma tap: 3 sines** (the swell cosine, the gamma envelope cosine, and the carrier). As for the gamma envelope, `|s - s_ideal| <= b / 2 + 2^-52` and `|e - e_ideal| <= b / 2 + 2^-52`. Each of the four depth-law operations adds at most `2^-53` on values in [0, 1], so `|a - a_ideal| <= d * b + 2^-49`, and `|gamma - gamma_ideal| <= 2 * b + 2^-48`, about -114 dBFS.
- **Stereo bus.** Without a theta reference it is the gamma tap (3 sines). With one it adds the theta tap's 2 sines, 5 in all: within `3.5 * b + 2^-46`, about -109 dBFS.
- **Output.** The binaural join adds that ear's binaural sine, 6 in all with both layers: within `4.5 * b + 2^-45`, about -107 dBFS.

Every bound is under row `reference-match` (-100 dBFS).

**Coupling timing.** Every swell peak lies at its coupling phase: in theta cycle `k` the swell peak is where `p_E = C`, at `t = (k + C / 2^64) / f_q` seconds, with `f_q` the theta rate the accumulator plays (Core, Frequency error). The coupling timing tool (`tests/assertions/coupling_timing.py`) measures it from the theta and gamma taps alone, within row `coupling-timing`.

**Level.** The gamma layer runs at unit amplitude; level fields arrive in Epic 4. The swell fixtures with both layers peak near 2.0, and their 24-bit WAVs clip by the 24-bit rounding rule. They are test fixtures, not for listening.

The coupling phase rule. `deg`, `x`, and `x * 2^64` are hex floats. The second and third rows are the ties `x * 2^64 = 1/2` and `3/2`, rounded to even; 360 degrees gives `C = 0`, the same as 0 degrees.

### Vectors: swell

The engine swell `s`, gamma envelope `e`, and coupled amplitude `a` (binary64 bits, from the engine sine bits) and their ideal values (25 significant digits: ideal cosines in decimal arithmetic, exact depth law on the binary64 `d`) at 0, 25, and 100 percent depth, for the coupling phases 0, 90, 180, and 270 degrees, at the theta envelope phases `C` (the swell peak), `C + 2^62`, and `C + 2^63` (the swell trough), each at the gamma envelope peak (phase 0) and trough (2^63). The swell does not depend on either rate.

### Vectors: swell fixtures

Per swell fixture and sample rate: the gamma carrier, rate, and depth, the coupling and coupling phase (hex floats) and `C`, the theta reference (`none` without a `theta_reference` field), the gamma carrier and envelope increments, the theta envelope increment `inc_E` (0 without a theta reference), and the session length `N` in frames. `swell-<shape>-<phase>deg-<depth>pct.json` is a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 40 Hz with a smooth swell at that coupling phase and peak depth, plus a theta reference: a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated at 6 Hz by that shape, 75 percent depth; 5 s, no binaural layer. `swell-gamma-depth-0-no-theta.json` has gamma depth 0 and no theta reference, which compile accepts. In this order the first four are the hash probe's `gamma-swell-<fs>` components.

Per swell fixture and sample rate at sample `n`: the gamma carrier and envelope phases and the theta envelope phase, the engine swell `s`, gamma envelope `e`, amplitude `a`, gamma tap, theta tap, and stereo bus (`theta + gamma`, one rounding; also the output, since there is no binaural layer) as binary64 bits, with the ideal swell, gamma tap, and bus (the exact sum of the ideal theta and gamma taps) at 25 digits. Indices cover the start (0 and 1), both sides of the first two block boundaries (63, 64, 65, 127, 128), sample 1000, both sides of the first swell peak after sample 0 and of the first swell trough (where `p_E` first reaches `C`, or first wraps when `C = 0`, and where it first reaches `C + 2^63`; none without a theta reference), both sides of the first gamma envelope trough, the last sample, and two samples past the end (case `past_end`: taps and output `+0.0`; the phase columns show `n * inc mod 2^64` only for reference).

## Binaural layer

Story 2.5 replaces the binaural oscillators stub (Stub chain) with the real layer: two pure sines, one per ear, the right carrier the left carrier plus the beat rate. With a fixed beat rate the beat rate is `beat_rate_hz`; with a beat rate cycle it follows the rate curve (Rate curve), so the right carrier is `carrier_left_hz + beat(t)`. The pair joins after every other stage (Stub chain, Stage order) and nothing reads it: no stage takes a binaural value as an input, and the gamma PAC layer never couples to it (AD-5; the binaural beat has no waveform in the file).

**Recipe fields.** In `binaural`:

- `carrier_left_hz`, required: the left carrier in Hz (range row `binaural-carrier`)
- `beat_rate_hz`, required: the beat rate in Hz (range row `beat-rate`); with a beat rate cycle it is the cycle's center `r`
- `beat_rate_cycle`, optional: an object with `swing_hz`, `period_s`, and `start_phase_deg`, all three required inside it (Rate curve, Recipe fields)
- `waveform`, optional: `sine` or `triangle`; absent means `sine`

Every recipe written before Story 2.5 has neither optional field, so its bytes and recipe hash are unchanged and it renders the stub's bits.

**Compile order.** The range checks walk the fields in this order and the first failure wins: `carrier_left_hz` (row `binaural-carrier`), `beat_rate_hz` (row `beat-rate`), then the derived right carrier `f_C = carrier_left_hz + beat_rate_hz` (one binary64 addition, row `binaural-carrier-right`, reported on `/binaural/beat_rate_hz`), then, with a beat rate cycle, the worst-case checks (Rate curve, Range checks). After every range check, compile runs the rules of the earlier sections (Theta reference, Gamma PAC layer, Coupling), then the waveform rule:

- **Non-sine binaural is not modeled.** `waveform` `triangle` is rejected: parameter `/binaural/waveform`, value `triangle`, rule `non-sine binaural not modeled`, limit `sine` (design log 2026-10-28 (o)). `sine` and an absent field pass, with the same bits. Any other text is a schema fault (rule `enum`). A non-sine binaural waveform does not exist until this section models it.

All of them run before `final.required`.

**Oscillators.** Three phase accumulators (Core), each at phase 0 at n = 0 except the cycle phase (Rate curve, Engine):

```
f_L   = carrier_left_hz
f_C   = carrier_left_hz + beat_rate_hz       binary64, one rounding: the right carrier at the center rate
inc_L = phase increment rule (f_L, fs)       left phase:   phase_L[n] = n * inc_L mod 2^64
inc_C = phase increment rule (f_C, fs)       center phase: phase_C[n] = n * inc_C mod 2^64
phase_R[n] = phase_C[n] + U[n] mod 2^64      right phase; U[n] = 0 without a beat rate cycle (Rate curve, Engine)
a_L = a_R = 1.0                              unit amplitude
```

The beat rate lives in the right ear only; the left ear is an unmodulated sine at `f_L` with or without a cycle. Without a cycle `phase_R = phase_C`, which is the stub's right phase (`f_R` of Stub chain is `f_C`), so the layer is the stub bit for bit and the stub chain vectors hold unchanged. A recipe without a `binaural` field has `a_L = a_R = 0.0` and `inc_L = inc_C = 0`, and no cycle.

**Muted ear.** As in Stub chain, Muted ear: the muted ear `c` has `inc_c = 0` and `a_c = 0.0`. A muted right ear also has no cycle, so `U = 0` and it outputs `0.0 * sine(0) = +0.0`. Muting changes nothing else.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < N` and each ear `c` (L or R), this line replaces the binaural oscillator line of Stub chain, Per sample; every other line there is unchanged:

```
bin[c][n] = a_c * sine(phase_c[n])      engine sine (Sine); phase_R from the line above
```

`join[c][n] = bus[c][n] + bin[c][n]` adds ear `c` to channel `c` only, after the stereo bus, and `master` and `out` follow it (Stub chain). There is no spatial, HRTF, ambisonic, reverb, or stereo-width stage in the chain, and none may be added between the binaural pair and the output.

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets `phase_L` to `k * inc_L mod 2^64`, `phase_C` to `k * inc_C mod 2^64`, and, with a cycle, the cycle phase to `P0 + k * inc_P mod 2^64` (Rate curve, Seek). `U` is a function of the cycle phase alone, so the right phase needs nothing else.

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/binaural.py`, implemented from this section and Rate curve with the rules of `reference/ideal.py`, never from engine code, engine binaries, or engine output) uses the same integer phases and increments and the ideal sine (Sine) of `phase_L`; it computes `P0` by the coupling phase rule itself. Without a cycle the right ear is the ideal sine of `phase_C`. With a cycle it computes `K = swing_hz * period_s / (2 * pi)` in binary64 (Python's `math.pi`), the ideal cosines of the signed phases `P0` and `p_P[n]` (in [-2^63, 2^63)) times `2 * pi / 2^64` with NumPy, `U = K * (c0 - c[n])` in cycles, then `y = phase_C[n] / 2^64 + U` in binary64, reduced to [-1/2, 1/2] by subtracting the nearest integer (`numpy.rint`), and the right ear is the NumPy sine of `2 * pi * y`. `reference/stub_chain.py` takes both ears from it.

**Error bound.** Write `e_t` for the engine sine's own error: the truncation bound `(pi/2)^13 / 13!` of Sine, Error bound plus rounding, below 5.7e-8 (about -144.9 dBFS; measured -145.0). Row `sine-error` (`b`, -120 dBFS) is the looser ceiling the sine is tested against. Write `e_c` for the cycle cosine's error (Rate curve, Cycle cosine), at most 1e-10 (row `cycle-cosine-error`, -200 dBFS). The margin of each tap is stated by its count of engine sine and cycle cosine evaluations per sample:

- **Fixed beat, each ear: 1 sine** (the carrier). `|bin - bin_ideal| <= e_t`, under `b`.
- **Beat rate cycle, right ear: 1 cycle cosine and 1 sine** (the cycle cosine and the carrier). The cycle cosine's error is scaled by `K` before it reaches the phase, which is why it is not the engine sine. With `eps_0 = |c0 - c0_ideal|` (0 at start phases 90 and 270 degrees, where `P0 + 2^62` folds to 0 and `c0 = +0.0` exactly; at most `e_c` otherwise), the right phase is within `delta = K * (eps_0 + e_c) + 2^-49 * K + 2^-64` cycles of the ideal integral (`2^-49 * K` covers the roundings of `K`, `c0 - c`, and `K * (c0 - c)`; `2^-64` the cycles-to-units rounding), and `|bin_R - bin_R_ideal| <= 2 * pi * delta + e_t`. The widest compile-accepted cycle has `K` below `15 * 60 / (2 * pi)`, about 143.3 (Rate curve, Range checks: `s <= r` and `r + s < 30`, at the 60 s period), so `2 * pi * delta` is at most about 1.8e-7 and the right ear is within about 2.4e-7 of its ideal, about -132 dBFS, at any start phase: 32 dB under row `reference-match`. For the Sleep cycle (`K` about 14.32) the bound is about -144 dBFS. The left ear is the fixed-beat case.
- **Output.** With no other layer the output is `bin` bit for bit (`+0.0 + bin = bin`). With the theta reference or the gamma PAC layer it adds that bus's bound (Theta reference, Gamma PAC layer, Coupling, Error bound) and one rounding of the join.

Every bound, at every compile-accepted cycle, is under row `reference-match` (-100 dBFS).

**Level.** Both ears run at unit amplitude; level fields arrive in Epic 4. A binaural-only fixture peaks near 1.0 in each channel. With another layer it peaks near 2.0 and its 24-bit WAV clips by the 24-bit rounding rule; such fixtures are for tests, not for listening.

### Vectors: binaural fixtures

Per binaural fixture and sample rate: the left carrier, the beat rate (the cycle center with a cycle), `f_C`, and the cycle's swing, period, and start phase (hex floats, `none` without a cycle), the left and center increments, `P0`, `inc_P`, and `K` (Rate curve, Engine; `none` without a cycle), and the session length `N` in frames. `binaural-beat-<rate>.json` is a 400 Hz pure sine in the left ear and a pure sine at 400 Hz plus that beat rate in the right ear, 5 s. `binaural-cycle-sleep.json` is a 100 Hz pure sine in the left ear and a pure sine in the right ear whose beat rate cycles over 0 to 3 Hz every 60 s from 0 Hz rising (the Sleep cycle, Rate curve), 120 s. None has another layer. `binaural-cycle-sleep.json` is the source of the hash probe's `binaural-cycle-<fs>` components.

### Vectors: binaural samples

Per binaural fixture and sample rate at sample `n`: the left, center, and cycle phases, the engine cycle cosine `c` (Rate curve, Cycle cosine) and `v = K * (c0 - c)` (binary64 bits; `none` without a cycle), `U` and the right phase, the left and right ears (binary64 bits, from the engine sine and cycle cosine bits; also the join, master, and output, since no other layer is present), and their ideal values at 25 digits: the ideal sine of `phase_L`, and of `phase_C / 2^64 + U_ideal` with `U_ideal = K_ideal * (cos(2 * pi * P0 / 2^64) - cos(2 * pi * p_P[n] / 2^64))` and the exact `K_ideal = s * P / (2 * pi)`. Indices cover the start (0 and 1), both sides of the first two block boundaries (63, 64, 65, 127, 128), sample 1000, the last sample, and two samples past the end (case `past_end`: the ears are `+0.0`; the phase columns are shown only for reference); for the cycle fixture also both sides of each quarter of the first cycle (where `n * inc_P` first reaches `k * 2^62`, k = 1 to 4: center rising, maximum, center falling, minimum) and both sides of the seek point at 55 s.

## Rate curve

Story 2.5 writes the one rate-curve law. Story 2.6 reuses it unchanged, with the same integral and the same conversion, for the theta and gamma rates. v0.1 has only this sinusoidal cycle: there are no piecewise-linear curves.

**Law.**

```
rate(t) = r + s * sin(2 * pi * t / P + phi0)
```

`r` is the center in Hz, `s >= 0` the swing in Hz, `P` the period in seconds, and `phi0` the start phase. A start phase of 0 degrees is at the center, rising; 90 degrees at the maximum `r + s`; 180 degrees at the center, falling; 270 degrees at the minimum `r - s`. The rate spans `[r - s, r + s]`.

**Recipe fields.** For the binaural beat rate: `r` is `binaural.beat_rate_hz`, and `binaural.beat_rate_cycle` holds the rest:

- `swing_hz`: `s`, at least 0 (no range row: the worst-case checks below bound it)
- `period_s`: `P` (range row `rate-cycle-period`, exactly 60 s)
- `start_phase_deg`: `phi0` in degrees (range row `rate-cycle-start-phase`, 0 to 360 inclusive; 360 is the same point as 0)

`period_s` is locked to 60 s, the only period with a source (Lee et al. 2024, design log 2026-10-27 (n)), until a study supports others.

**Range checks, worst case.** With a cycle, right after the binaural checks of Binaural layer, Compile order, and in this order (first failure wins):

1. `swing_hz >= 0`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value the swing, rule `range.min`, limit `>= 0 Hz`.
2. The lowest rate `r - s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `beat-rate`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value `r - s`, for example limit `>= 0 Hz (row beat-rate)`.
3. The highest rate `r + s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `beat-rate`: same parameter, value `r + s`, for example limit `< 30 Hz (row beat-rate)`.
4. The highest right carrier `f_C + s` (binary64, one rounding, with `f_C` from Binaural layer, Oscillators) against row `binaural-carrier-right`: same parameter, value `f_C + s`, limit `< 1030 Hz (row binaural-carrier-right)`.
5. `period_s` against row `rate-cycle-period`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/period_s`, rule `range.min` or `range.max` (59.99 and 60.01 are both rejected).
6. `start_phase_deg` against row `rate-cycle-start-phase`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/start_phase_deg`.

A cycle field that is not a number, a missing one, or an unknown one is a schema fault (rules `type`, `required`, `unknown_property`).

**Sleep cycle.** `r = 1.5`, `s = 1.5`, `P = 60`, `phi0 = 270` degrees: the beat rate cycles over 0 to 3 Hz every 60 s (Lee et al. 2024, design log 2026-10-27 (n)) and starts at 0 Hz, rising, so a session opens with no beat and the beat eases in. The start phase is an engineering default chosen by Audio on 2026-11-11; no study sets it (Lee et al. 2024 does not say). The Sleep starter recipe itself is not part of this story.

**Phase integral.** The beat phase is the integral of the rate, in cycles:

```
ideal:  Phi(t) = r * t + K * (cos(phi0) - cos(2 * pi * t / P + phi0)),    K = s * P / (2 * pi)
```

so `Phi(0) = 0` and `dPhi/dt = rate(t)`. The right ear phase is `f_L * t + Phi(t) = (f_L + r) * t + U(t)`, with `U(t) = K * (cos(phi0) - cos(2 * pi * t / P + phi0))`: the center term is an ordinary accumulator and `U` is one closed-form value per sample. No phase is summed from per-sample increments of a changing rate.

**Engine.** Once per plan:

```
P0    = coupling phase rule (start_phase_deg)                (Coupling, Coupling phase rule; 360 gives 0)
q_P   = 1.0 / period_s                                        binary64, one rounding
inc_P = phase increment rule (q_P, fs)                        cycle phase increment
K     = (swing_hz * period_s) * 0x1.45f306dc9c883p-3         two roundings; the constant is the binary64 nearest 1 / (2 pi)
c0    = cycle_cosine(P0)                                      the cosine of the start phase (Cycle cosine)
```

Per sample, in this order:

```
p_P[n]     = P0 + n * inc_P mod 2^64       cycle phase (P0 at n = 0)
c[n]       = cycle_cosine(p_P[n])          the cosine of the cycle phase (Cycle cosine)
v[n]       = K * (c0 - c[n])               two binary64 operations: c0 - c, then K times it
U[n]       = cycles-to-units rule (v[n])
phase_R[n] = phase_C[n] + U[n] mod 2^64    (Binaural layer, Oscillators)
```

At n = 0, `c[0]` is `c0` bit for bit (one function of the same phase), so `v[0] = K * +0.0 = +0.0`, `U[0] = 0`, and the right ear starts at phase 0 like the left. Every value is a pure function of `n`: two integer accumulators and one engine cosine.

**Cycle cosine.** `U` multiplies the cosine's error by `K`, up to about 143, so the cycle uses its own cosine, more exact than the engine sine (Sine, Engine sine), for both `c0` and `c[n]`. For a phase `p`:

1. **Quarter turn and fold, exact integer steps.** `q = p + 2^62 mod 2^64` (the cosine of `p` is the sine of `q`), then the engine sine's step 1 on `q`: read it as a signed 64-bit integer `s`, and fold into `[-2^62, 2^62]` with `s = 2^63 - s` above 2^62 and `s = -2^63 - s` below -2^62.
2. **Convert once.** `x = double(s) * 0x1.921fb54442d18p-62`, the engine sine's step 2.
3. **Odd Taylor polynomial to x^15, fixed Horner order.** With the coefficients of Vectors: cycle cosine coefficients (`Dk` the binary64 nearest to `+-1/k!`, sign alternating, `+` for k = 1; `D1` to `D11` equal the engine sine's `C1` to `C11`):

   ```
   z = x * x
   r = D15
   r = r * z + D13
   r = r * z + D11
   r = r * z + D9
   r = r * z + D7
   r = r * z + D5
   r = r * z + D3
   r = r * z + D1
   y = r * x
   ```

   Each `*` and `+` is one binary64 operation (no fused multiply-add).

**Error bound.** `|cycle_cosine(p) - cos(2 * pi * p / 2^64)| <= e_c = 1e-10` (row `cycle-cosine-error`, -200 dBFS), tested at the phases of Sine, Test resolution. The truncation error of the polynomial on `[-pi/2, pi/2]` is at most `(pi/2)^17 / 17!`, about 6.1e-12 (-224 dBFS), and the roundings add about 1e-16, so the bound holds with about 24 dB to spare. At phases 2^62 and 3 * 2^62 the fold gives `s = 0` and `y = +0.0` exactly, so `c0 = +0.0` at start phases 90 and 270 degrees. The engine sine is unchanged; only the beat rate cycle uses the cycle cosine.

**Cycles-to-units rule.** A value `v` in cycles (binary64) becomes phase units, `(v mod 1) * 2^64` rounded:

```
if not (|v| < 2^52):  U = 0         NaN, both infinities, and |v| >= 2^52 (an integer, so 0 mod 1)
w = trunc(v)                        the integer part, toward zero; |v| < 2^52, so it fits a signed 64-bit integer
f = v - w                           binary64, exact: the fraction bits of v, in (-1, 1), with the sign of v
x = f * 0x1p64                      binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale)
U = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even, modulo 2^64
```

`f` is exact because it keeps only bits `v` already has. `|x| <= (1 - 2^-53) * 2^64 < 2^64`, so the rounded magnitude fits an unsigned 64-bit integer and no conversion can overflow. Rounding ties to even is symmetric, so a negative `x` gives `U = 2^64 - round(-x)` (and 0 where `round(-x)` is 0): `U` is the same point of the cycle as the exact `v mod 1`, within half a unit. Ties can happen only for `|x| < 2^52` (Vectors: cycles to units).

**Frequency.** The instantaneous rate the right ear plays is `f_C + dU/dt`. The accumulators play `f_C` and `q_P` within the increment rule's frequency error (Core, Frequency error), so the center rate is off by under 1e-13 Hz and the cycle period by under 1e-11 s; the cycle cosine's error in `U` (Binaural layer, Error bound) changes only as slowly as the cycle phase. So the instantaneous rate follows the schedule within row `frequency-accuracy`, and the phase is continuous: from one sample to the next `U` changes by at most `s / fs` cycles plus the rounding, with no step. The frequency tool's phase mode (`tests/assertions/frequency.py`) measures both from the right ear tap.

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets the cycle phase to `P0 + k * inc_P mod 2^64` and the center phase to `k * inc_C mod 2^64`; `U` needs nothing else, so seek is one step for any `k` and the block size cannot change a sample.

### Vectors: cycle cosine coefficients

`Dk`, the binary64 nearest to `+-1/k!`, as hex floats. `D1` to `D11` equal the engine sine's coefficients.

| Term | Exact | Binary64 (hex float) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| x^1 | +1/1! | `0x1.0000000000000p+0` |
| x^3 | -1/3! | `-0x1.5555555555555p-3` |
| x^5 | +1/5! | `0x1.1111111111111p-7` |
| x^7 | -1/7! | `-0x1.a01a01a01a01ap-13` |
| x^9 | +1/9! | `0x1.71de3a556c734p-19` |
| x^11 | -1/11! | `-0x1.ae64567f544e4p-26` |
| x^13 | +1/13! | `0x1.6124613a86d09p-33` |
| x^15 | -1/15! | `-0x1.ae7f3e733b81fp-41` |

### Vectors: cycle cosine

The engine cycle cosine (binary64 bits) at the quadrant edges and their neighbours, at eighths of a cycle, and at the Sleep cycle phase of sample 1000 at 48 kHz, with the ideal cosine at 25 digits.

| Cycle cosine case | Phase | Cycle cosine (binary64 bits) | Cosine ideal |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 0 | `0x3fefffffffff2c12` | 1 |
| 1 | 1 | `0x3fefffffffff2c12` | 1 |
| 2^60 | 1152921504606846976 | `0x3fed906bcf328bae` | 0.9238795325112867561281832 |
| 2^61 | 2305843009213693952 | `0x3fe6a09e667f3bcc` | 0.7071067811865475244008444 |
| 2^62 - 1 | 4611686018427387903 | `0x3c1921fb54442d18` | 3.406121580086554589335196e-19 |
| 2^62 | 4611686018427387904 | `0x0000000000000000` | 0 |

## 24-bit rounding

The command-line renderer (Story 1.4) writes the render output (Stub chain, Output and taps) as 24-bit PCM. Each binary64 output sample `s` becomes one signed 24-bit integer `v` by this rule. There is no dither and no other shaping.

```
if s is NaN:            v = 0                      any sign or payload
x = s * 0x1p23          binary64, exact (see below)
if x >= 8388607:        v = 8388607                +1.0, everything above full scale, +inf
else if x <= -8388608:  v = -8388608               -1.0, everything below, -inf
else:                   v = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even
```

`v` is written as 3 bytes, little-endian two's complement: the bytes of `v mod 2^24`, least significant first. Samples are written in render order, left then right per frame.

**Exactness.** Scaling by 2^23 is exact for every finite `s`: it only changes the exponent, a subnormal `s` moves toward the normal range and loses no bits, and a product too large for binary64 becomes an infinity of the same sign, which the clamp treats the same as the exact value. The comparisons are exact. In the last branch `|x| < 2^23`, so `x` and its fractional part `x - floor(x)` are exact binary64 values, and the tie test compares that part with exactly 1/2. No step depends on the host rounding mode or denormal mode: a subnormal `s` gives `v = 0` whether or not the host flushes it.

**Range.** The 24-bit range is asymmetric: +1.0 maps to 8388607 (2^23 - 1), and -1.0 to -8388608 (-2^23). Signed zeros and every `|x| < 1/2` give 0; `|x|` exactly 1/2 also gives 0 (ties to even).

### Vectors: 24-bit rounding

`s` and `x` are hex floats (`nan`, `inf`, and `-inf` as written). Rules: `clamp` (at or past full scale), `nan`, `tie` (`x` exactly k + 1/2), `round` (nearest, not a tie), and `zero` (signed zeros and values that round to 0 without a tie). Bytes are the 3 written bytes in file order, as hex.

| Rule | Case | s (hex) | x = s * 2^23 (hex) | Value | Bytes (little-endian) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| clamp | +1.0 | `0x1.0000000000000p+0` | `0x1.0000000000000p+23` | 8388607 | `ffff7f` |
| clamp | next binary64 above +1.0 | `0x1.0000000000001p+0` | `0x1.0000000000001p+23` | 8388607 | `ffff7f` |
| clamp | +2.0 | `0x1.0000000000000p+1` | `0x1.0000000000000p+24` | 8388607 | `ffff7f` |
| clamp | +infinity | `inf` | `inf` | 8388607 | `ffff7f` |

| clamp | x exactly 8388607 | `0x1.fffffc0000000p-1` | `0x1.fffffc0000000p+22` | 8388607 | `ffff7f` |
| clamp | -1.0 (x exactly -8388608) | `-0x1.0000000000000p+0` | `-0x1.0000000000000p+23` | -8388608 | `000080` |
| clamp | next binary64 below -1.0 | `-0x1.0000000000001p+0` | `-0x1.0000000000001p+23` | -8388608 | `000080` |
| clamp | -2.0 | `-0x1.0000000000000p+1` | `-0x1.0000000000000p+24` | -8388608 | `000080` |
